**Status:** PROPOSED (L0 contracts reconciler). This is the single execution queue for lanes A–F. **No owner approval is recorded here**; every gate keeps its state in [owner-decisions](../../capstone-report/owner-decisions-2026-09-27.md).
**Refresh Trigger:** any item below changing status; any merge of #95, #97, #99, #82, #84, #74, #72, #94 or #68; any owner answer to a gate listed here; any edit to [contracts.md](contracts.md).
**Base measured:** `origin/main` @ `3b2236c` (2026-10-15); collected slot 1574.

Rules: [contracts.md](contracts.md) §b (shared files), §c (migrations), §d (count slots). Path shorthand: `be/` = `src/backend/`, `fe/` = `src/frontend/src/`; "slots" = the `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENT.md` collected-count lines, which are exempt from the no-shared-file rule (§b rule 3).

**Scheduling rules**

1. **Code slots:** at most 12 code items run at once (box-wide, all lanes; `build-wave` default `max`; owner cap 30 agents, 2026-10-16). Every pytest run goes through `scripts/pyslot.sh` (3 machine-wide slots on the 22 GiB WSL box). Lane docs that say "at most 2 code L1s" are superseded by this rule.
2. **No shared files:** two items that run at the same time never share a file (slots, `docs/INDEX.md` and `docs/_link_graph.json` excepted).
3. **Waves are dependency layers**, not dates. An item may start in an earlier wave if its dependencies are merged, its execution gates are answered, a slot is free and rules 1–2 hold.
4. **No-slot items** (docs, owner merges, PR landings with no pytest) run beside code items, subject to rule 2.
5. Humans merge, in any order that respects real code dependencies. Slots and the index are updated by L0's post-merge batch (contracts §d, SLOT-RULE-2).

  - **UNCERTAINTY-VERIFY** (*planned 2026-10-20, second batch: row UNCERTAINTY-VERIFY; new gates UNC-VERIFY-SCOPE, UNC-VERIFY-DIFF; correction: `extract_claims` skips uncertainty sections before `filter_unverifiable_claims` runs (`claim_extractor.py:130-132`), so both sites change*) (found 2026-10-20, #215 / LCP-HL-DIFF disclosure (5)): uncertainty sections are never verified, so a false value in an UNCERTAINTY / UNCERTAINTIES section after verified facts is served (pre-existing for the plural; disclosed in LCP-HL-DIFF, known gap kept per LCP-HL-SCOPE (a)). Touches `ClaimExtractor.filter_unverifiable_claims`. Never beside B-3.

Accepted as Task 0 proposed: the refusal message goes to the log only (`core/auth.py` stays off limits); the snapshot is named `vault.pre017.snapshot`, never ends in `.db`, with a test that a real backup leaves it out; time set explicitly and compared with `core.time`; temp name, fsync, verify with the key and `quick_check`, then `os.replace`; an unversioned vault is snapshotted before `stamp`; schema match compares tables, columns and FK actions, with a test that all 17 fingerprints differ; `/test/reset` purges explicitly; tests in `test_fk_migration_017.py` or `test_fk_enforcement.py`; P8 and P12 join `_REPAIR_SET` (4 repair checks and 14 delete checks). The PR body shows the owner in the final diff: the withdrawn rotation purge and why; the new recovery trigger (`recover_profile` commits the new password and rotated code before opening the vault, `:791`, so a refused snapshot returns 500 and that new code is lost); and that strict matching can refuse a real pre-Alembic vault that matches no revision (login returns 500, data untouched; no such vault exists to measure). **L0 ruling on #454 (comment 6603412162, 2026-11-03, Codex's three findings on `2d470854`):** all three are fixed, in `core/migrations.py` and its tests, plus `docs/compliance/data-privacy.md` for the retention wording; never `core/audit.py` or `core/auth.py`. (1) The snapshot's age purge moves to after the migration succeeds and the vault is confirmed at revision 017 or later; a vault already at head still purges on open; a refused or failed migration keeps the snapshot at any age (a vault that cannot complete 017 keeps its snapshot past 30 days, until it completes or the profile is deleted; L0 lists this for the owner); clearing the interrupted temp copy may stay where it is. (2) A snapshot that fails verification is never overwritten and never deleted: it and its sidecars move aside, byte for byte, to one fixed name, then a new snapshot is taken as today; if that aside name is occupied, refuse with the existing log-only refusal, touching nothing; the aside file falls under the same 30-day purge, `purge_pre017_snapshot` and profile deletion (`_snapshot_paths` or its replacement covers it), and a sweep reports anything else that enumerates the vault directory or these names. (3) "Non-empty" means any user table, excluding `alembic_version` and the derived search-index tables the schema match already ignores (`96dab82d`): every non-empty unversioned file goes through `_match_unversioned_vault`, and no match means refuse with no write and no stamp; this is inside P6-STAMP. Tests RED first, stored state read back, with deliberate breaks; then the full suite, the security-lens review and a Codex adversarial review of the new head (a new Codex `[high]` goes to L0 with a check of it, not a loop). The owner ask in the PR body is rewritten at the final head with the exact production diff and two new consequences (the retention extension in 1 and the aside file in 2), and states that power loss and disk damage were not measured and that the orphan evidence is synthetic. **Won't plan (2026-10-19):**
- **DL-EMB-RACE** (#196 Codex finding 2). The races need a second local actor that creates an empty target directory, or deletes and recreates `.partial`, during a developer-run fetch on a single-user box; such an actor can already replace the model files directly. The dangling-symlink half does not hold on Linux (errno 20, measured by the #196 L1), and W-8-HARDEN's Design 3 chose exclusive `mkdir` ownership on purpose (HC-EMB-004c/d/e). Reopen if the fetch ever runs unattended or shared.
- ~~MSET-EXT-NOKEY-MINORS~~: **moved to a row** (MSET-EXT-NOKEY-PIN) after PR #202 review round 1. The first pass said minor 1 did not reproduce, but its scratch mutant had dropped the test's reference to `row`. With `row` held, as HC-AUDCMS-005 does, a raw-SQL key wipe in request 2 passes every assertion (measured on `5750a97`).
- **Folded, not separate rows:** EMB-HARDEN-MINORS and REPROCESS-007C-REACH are tasks of EMB-LOAD-LOG-PATH. The "LLM model paths at INFO" part of EMB-LOAD-LOG-PATH is its own row, LLM-PATH-LOG, because its files are held by #185 and B-5.

Task 1 first pass was INCONCLUSIVE (comment 6593328014, 2026-11-02): 0 `database is locked` in 50 loads (40 baseline, 10 forced) outside the positive control, but no proven overlap: in all 10 forced loads the forced backup finished (+0.22 to +0.32 s after the sentinel) before the page's first audited commit (+0.34 to +0.46 s); not a NOT REPRODUCED verdict, and the row does not close on it. L0 ruling (comment 6593346631): one more forced phase, licensed design change in Task 1 (no product code, synthetic data only, same logging limits, through `quiet_run.sh`): repeat only the forced phase and the positive control, 10 loads minimum, with the forced backup started so its interval overlaps the audited writes (for example a sentinel touched about 300 ms after `page.goto`), logging the overlap per load; the stored master stays at its natural size and the backup gets no artificial delay. Decision rule: at least one proven overlap and 0 locks in all forced loads means NOT REPRODUCED and the row closes won't-fix under its own rule (a hold over 5 s by any writer still gives the HTTP 500 the control measured; the F-9 log line was never kept); any `database is locked` outside the control means REPRODUCED, name the holder and stop (Task 2 needs DB-LOCK-FIX); 10 more loads with no overlap: report it and stop. L0 edit 2026-11-02: Task 1's runtime cell read "L0 or claude", which build-item refuses (RUNBOOK §1 rule 1); it now reads `claude`. Nothing else in the row changed; runtime cell set to exactly `claude` 2026-11-02 (build-item's runtime check is literal; Task 1 still runs on a box with the app) |

Qwen3-4B Q4_K_M pp512 120.7 tok/s (4 threads) and 121.7 (8 threads), tg128 14.50 and 14.34; Phi-4-mini Q4_K_M pp512 127.5 and 127.7, tg128 15.01 and 15.11. Still open, so the row stays **partly met**: ~~`llama-bench`~~ (done as measured 2026-11-06, below, including the 12 and 24 thread follow-up) and the 2026-10-22 judge set. **llama-bench done 2026-11-06 (BATCH-1106C).** Owner, in chat (verbatim as L0 relayed it): "Run Haiku 5.5 research on prebuilt?"; a Haiku 5.5 xhigh research agent ran read-only with no download. Owner answer LLAMA-BENCH-DL (gate name assigned by L0), AskUserQuestion, verbatim label "Yes, download and run it (Recommended)" ([owner-decisions](../../capstone-report/owner-decisions-2026-09-27.md) row LLAMA-BENCH-DL). Prebuilt release b10453 = llama.cpp `3cb7ffb1`; the vendored llama-cpp-python 0.3.35 pins `4df29be4` (b10453 is that plus one CI-only commit); the asset sha256 (`550eb155...59fb4`) was verified on download. Run: CPU only, `-ngl 0 -fa off`, `-r 3`, CPU backend `libggml-cpu-haswell.so`, load 1.41 at start, prechecks passed. Qwen3-4B pp512 33.80±0.81 (4 threads) and 58.08±1.92 (8), tg128 7.29±0.30 and 8.29±0.50; Phi-4-mini pp512 38.70±1.50 and 58.87±1.13, tg128 8.10±0.52 and 8.82±0.08 (tok/s). Evidence: section "llama-bench b10453, 2026-11-06" of [docs/reviews/2026-10-28/qwen3-4b-tier.md](../../reviews/2026-10-28/qwen3-4b-tier.md); logs `~/hc-quiet-logs/qwen3-4b-tier/llama-bench-r1106` on the model host. **Correction:** the `qwen_tier.py` pp512 figures (2026-10-28 and the 2026-11-06 quiet-box re-run) ran prompt processing at `n_threads_batch` = 24 (llama-cpp-python's default, `cpu_count`), so their "threads" column applies to generation only, and the llama-bench pp numbers are not comparable with them. The tg gap at 8 threads (8.3 against 14.3 for Qwen3-4B) is unexplained. **Follow-up at 12 and 24 threads (done, `llama_bench_t24.sh`, both lines EXIT=0, "lines expected=2 bad=0"):** Qwen3-4B pp512 79.60±5.52 (12) and 53.82±4.03 (24), tg128 6.97±0.24 and 1.57±0.29; Phi-4-mini pp512 70.07±2.04 and 61.96±5.17, tg128 9.30±0.07 and 2.32±0.08. Reading, untested: at matched threads the release llama-bench (generic runtime-selected `libggml-cpu-haswell.so` kernels) is slower than the local native llama-cpp-python build (pp512 70 to 80 against 121 to 128); tg collapses at 24 threads (SMT oversubscription); background agent load may have lowered the numbers. The numbers are not comparable one-for-one with the `qwen_tier.py` figures; the model ranking under llama-bench is close (Phi-4-mini slightly faster at tg, Qwen3-4B slightly faster at pp at 12 threads). No further runs are planned and no recommendation is made. |

No separate row.

## Planned 2026-10-23, frontend revamp

Planned 2026-10-23 on `15fe095` (after #310 merged) from the owner's frontend direction and prompt 44 (L0 AskUserQuestion; L0 evidence comment 6374428883 on PR #311) and prompt 45 (L0 evidence comment 6374428087 on PR #311); owner-decisions rows UI-DIRECTION, UI-SAAS-MEANING, UI-SCOPE-1031, UI-DARK-MODE, UI-SAFETY-TOGGLE, UI-DEPS, UI-FIXTURE-NAME land in the same PR as this section, merging two independent designer proposals. **Plan:** one file, [2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md](../2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md) (each row links its section); research package [docs/research/2026-10-23-frontend-revamp/](../../research/2026-10-23-frontend-revamp/00-README.md) (proposals, comparison, prototype patches, screenshots). **No owner approval is recorded here.** **Answers used:** UI-SAAS-MEANING "Craft + bold showcase"; UI-SCOPE-1031 "Full plan, no freeze" (L0 reading: every row is queued now and starts when its own dependencies merge; tokens and shell first; each page migrates completely in its row); UI-DARK-MODE "Follow OS setting" (light until UI-DARK-FLIP, which is gated on a no-unmigrated-colour test); UI-SAFETY-TOGGLE "Make it real" (research row SAFETY-MODE-REAL; until a build row lands the toggle stays, relabelled, plan §3.6); UI-DEPS "Keep framer-motion 10" (no `motion` 14; the other listed packages approved; any other major back to the owner); UI-FIXTURE-NAME "Replace with fixture" (applied in the research package). **Answered in prompt 48 (L0 evidence comment 6380025171 on PR #311):** UI-ROUTE-MOTION "Whole-page fade" (every page fades opacity .35→1, ~200 ms, values included; the plan makes it opacity-only, ≤ 240 ms, then still), UI-AUTH-SCREENS "Layout, motion and Lift" (UI-PG-AUTH and UI-PG-SECURITY no longer gated), SESSION-LOCK-NOW "Add Lock now" (SESSION-REENTRY Task 3 ready once UI-SHELL merges; Task 1 merged as #312). **New gates (open, not asked):** SAFETY-MODE-BEHAVIOUR (after SAFETY-MODE-REAL), SAFETY-MODE-DIFF (per ask-first file of the chosen behaviour); non-blocking open questions SAFETY-LABEL-WORDING, UI-NAV-NAMES, UI-BRAND-MARK, MED-BADGES, UI-ABOUT-PLACE, INK-MOMENT; drafts in the plan's [§11](../2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md#11-owner-gates-and-open-questions). **Ask-first files:** none edited by any row here (SAFETY-MODE-REAL is research only; no `UI-*` row edits a backend file). **Acceptance for every `UI-*` row:** gate G (axe 0 serious/critical in light and dark, CLS < 0.1, INP proxy < 200 ms at CPU ×4, bundle budgets, reduced-motion snapshot, no horizontal scroll at 390 and 320, zero console errors and no non-localhost request) plus G10, the stillness probe on every `[data-t0]` element (effective opacity and bounding rect; fails on zero nodes; owners of `data-t0` in plan §2.7), plus the defect checks DF-1…DF-6 its section names. **Motion rules:** every page change is the owner's opacity-only route fade (UI-ROUTE-MOTION "Whole-page fade", ≤ 240 ms, then still; under reduced motion instant); overlays holding T0 content open with M0 (L0 reading). **e2e contract changes:** every `src/frontend/e2e/*.spec.ts` assertion a row changes is listed old → new in plan [Appendix A](../2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md#appendix-a-e2e-assertions-and-the-row-that-changes-each) and in that row's files ([plan §6](../2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md#6-standard-acceptance-gate-and-defect-checks)). **Shared-file rules:** `src/frontend/package.json` only in UI-MOTION, UI-BUDGET, UI-CHART-CLEANUP (never beside AXE-E2E or another `package.json` PR); `fe/App.tsx` only in UI-ROUTES, UI-PG-RESULTS, UI-OVERVIEW, UI-ABOUT (different waves); `fe/components/ui/index.ts` only in UI-PRIM-CONTROLS. **Overlaps with MVP rows** (plan [§9](../2026-10-23-frontend-revamp-plan.md#9-reconciliation-with-rows-already-on-main)): UI-MOTION is never beside SESSION-REENTRY Task 1, FE-IMPORT-ERROR, IMPORT-SCANNED-PAGE, FE-VERIFY-NUMERIC, FE-EXPLAIN-ERR or BADGE-TICK (L0 order: those first); page rows depend on the MVP row that owns their page. **Rows planned: 31** (30 `UI-*` + SAFETY-MODE-REAL) plus SESSION-REENTRY Task 3 (a delta to the existing row, not a new row). **MVP priority** as in the MVP section (P0 safety/privacy/lab-value claim false or phone unusable; P1 demo path; P2 after).

Try again." No write in the GET. Test first, through `route_client`: a stored pending row at zero bytes, and a stored downloading row with bytes, both read back `failed`, and a new POST is accepted; a row with a live key stays active. Frontend test, RED today: fresh load with that failed answer gives Download enabled and no bar, and a press reaches the POST. Each must go red under a named break. The four T0 statements render in the first commit and are the same nodes afterwards. The `min-h-screen` slot keeps the page scrollable from the first paint, so a classic scrollbar cannot shift the column. Diagnosis: a dev trace of a miss showed React render slices of 6.4, 2.7 and 14 ms, then a frame with a 4.6 ms layout of 178 objects, all inside one sampler gap. **The bound stays at 40 ms:** the owner left the choice to L0 ("#499 - whichever is best (your verdict)", owner-decisions row ABOUT-499-BOUND), and L0 chose to fix the page rather than apply the 70 ms page-change bound; L0 brings numbers to the owner if it still misses. G5 frame `about-light-reduced.png` at this head shows the whole ribbon drawn, every point hollow ("Not yet checked"). The merge waits on this ruling reaching main (rule 8). **Accepted at an earlier head** (not repeated): the five "For L0" items of ruling 6665513206 above are unchanged. **L0 ruling on #504 (comment 6677202618, 2026-11-06; BATCH-1105E): licence for a test-only fix, merged as #504 `18a4bd12` (SHA checked on `origin/main`).** HC-UIABT-002 failed 3 of 3 recent PR merge-ref runs (#486, #503) with opacity 0.15-0.19, because #499's first-paint change (`e258f381`) makes the S1 wrappers mount and start fading before `renderAbout()` returns. The fix reads each wrapper's starting opacity in the microtask after it mounts. The assertion stays exactly '0', and a new size check means it cannot pass without seeing every group. Evidence in the PR body, as the ruling states it: the old test fails with an added delay, the new one passes 20 of 20, and the new one goes red when the wrappers start visible. File changed: `fe/__tests__/ui-about.test.tsx` (the only file in #504's diff). No product code. |

A backend row adds per-point ranges, then per-point wording. **BATCH-1111G (measured on `c79b7fd1`):** `test_probe_trend_point_carries_own_range` FAILED `assert None == 12.0` with two reports whose ranges are 12-16 and 13.5-17.5. `TrendPoint` has no bounds (`be/api/observations.py:215-234`, `fe/services/types.ts:194-212`) while the series band combines the last bounds seen (`:798-806`, `:842-856`), so a point is judged against another report's range. G9 and `safetyVisible` check opacity, not occlusion, so no test would catch it. Candidate fix: a mobile bottom offset of `var(--shell-bottom-h)`. Near-misses to extend: HC-UISH-005 (`e2e/ui-shell.spec.ts:395-422`) hit-tests the rail with no toast showing; HC-UISH-007 (`:789`) checks a toast against the More sheet only. Measure first.

- **MEDS-G-FLAKE** (found 2026-11-11, L0's G3 reports; candidate, not built unasked; **diagnosed by BATCH-1111G: harness timing, not ICU**): `e2e/ui-meds.spec.ts:254` on `/medications?tab=reminders` (stillness) and `med-fixture-1` in dark mode (axe colour-contrast mid-transition) fail the G3 job on unrelated PRs (#591 twice, #602, #610). **Cause (report `~/hc-quiet-logs/staging-1111c/meds-g-diagnosis.md`, off-repo; no fix built):** two separate harness-timing failures share the one test; neither is ICU and neither is a G3 (INP) failure (the job is named "G3 on Production Build", but the assertions that fail are G10 and G1). (1) **G10 sampling gap on the Reminders tab (5 of the 6 CI hits).** The tab click runs 8-20 ms of synchronous main-thread work (Radix Tabs activates on `mousedown`, then `setSearchParams` triggers a react-router navigation and a React render of the tab panel). That lands when the probe's 20 ms `setInterval` tick is due, so the tick runs late. On the quiet box the largest gap has a median of 30 ms and a p90 of 38 ms against a 40 ms bound (`kind: 'interaction'`); CI's 41, 44 and 41 ms are the tail. No stillness property failed; only the sampler's coverage check. (2) **G1 axe colour-contrast in dark (1 of the 6 CI hits, `med-fixture-1`).** `setTheme` flips `.dark` and axe starts at once while 33 CSS colour transitions (150/200 ms) are still running, so axe reads a blend of the two themes (10/20 when axe starts at once, 0/20 after the transitions finish). The prior ICU hypothesis (a first `toLocaleTimeString`, as in UIDOC-006A) is **refuted**: in 22 traced runs, 20 have 0.0 ms of `toLocale*` time in the gap window, and the warm-up A/B does not move the gap (30 to 30, 32 to 32, 32 to 31 ms median). Fix before the owner makes G3 required, or required G3 blocks unrelated PRs. **Owner gate MEDS-G-X1: OPEN (asked, not answered).** Draft question, verbatim from the report: **Done (BATCH-1112C):** #621 merged `7c7ab592`, #640 merged `dddf95b5`; history row MEDS-G-FLAKE in "Planned 2026-11-12, batch 1112C".

  > Q: "MEDS-G-FLAKE, measured: `ui-meds:254` fails CI for two harness-timing reasons, not ICU (the warm-up A/B made no
  > difference). (1) On the Reminders tab the stillness sampler's 20 ms timer is delayed by the tab-switch render
  > (8-20 ms), so its largest gap is 30 ms median against a 40 ms bound; CI's 41-44 ms are the tail. Nothing on screen
  > moved. (2) In dark, axe runs while the theme's 150-200 ms colour transitions are still blending (10/20 when axe
  > starts at once, 0/20 after they finish). Which fix?"
  >
  > - **"Harness: rAF samples + settle theme (Recommended)"**: "`gates.ts` only. The stillness sampler also samples on
  >   each animation frame (the 40 ms bound stays), and axe waits for running transitions after a theme change (the
  >   HC-DPC-003 pattern). Measured 1/40 → 0/40 and 10/20 → 0/20. Applies to every UI gate spec; Task 2 re-runs them all."
  > - **"Tab switch counts as a page change (70 ms)"**: "Reminders tab uses the STILLNESS-NAV-70 bound; axe settle as
  >   above. Every measured gap ≤ 63 ms."
  > - **"Product: startTransition on the tab switch"**: "Makes the render yield; unmeasured (estimate median ~25 ms);
  >   axe settle as above. Product UI change."
  > - **"Known flake, re-run on red"**: "No change; G3 cannot be made a required check."

  Fix options from the report (all measured on the quiet box, `HEAD 15b03eb1`, `--workers=1 --retries=0`):

  | # | Change | Files | Expected effect | Owner gate |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | **A (Recommended)** | Harness: (1) G1 waits for finite animations after `setTheme`, then one frame, before axe (the `settleTheme`/`settleFiniteAnimations` already in `ui-dark-primitives.spec.ts:15-32`, HC-DPC-003, moved into `gates.ts`). (2) The G10 sampler also samples on every `requestAnimationFrame` while running, and keeps the 20 ms interval. No bound moves. | `e2e/support/gates.ts` only | G10 **measured** 1/40 to 0/40 at x1, median 30 to 17 ms, max 41 to 27 ms; x2 max 33 (n=8, partial). G1 **measured** 10/20 to 0/20 (diag order). | Not required by rule (no bound, no product UI). Owner ack advised: it changes how two shared gates measure on every UI gate spec. |
  | B | Bound: classify the Reminders tab trigger as a page change (70 ms under STILLNESS-NAV-70, or a new row), plus A(1) for G1. | `ui-meds.spec.ts`, plus `gates.ts` for G1 | G10 estimated 0 misses (every measured gap is 63 ms or less; CI max 44). | **Yes** (bound) |
  | C | Product: wrap the tab change (`MedicationCoach.tsx:196` `setSearchParams`) in `startTransition`; G1 still needs A(1). | `MedicationCoach.tsx` + `gates.ts` | **Not measured.** Estimate: median gap about 25 ms. Risk: the old panel stays up during the transition, which G10/G5 would need to re-check. | **Yes** (product UI) |
  | D | Known flake: re-run on red (the /export UIDOC-006A precedent). | none | No change: the CI rate stays at 6 hits in a few days. **Blocks making G3 required** (HANDOFF line 84). | Yes |

  Not noticed by the report: option A was measured as an init-script A/B, not as the real `gates.ts` change (pr2 at x2 has 8 runs only); Task 2 must re-measure the real change at x1 and x2 and run the other G10/G1 gate specs. The cost of an rAF sample on heavier pages is unmeasured.
  - **Fix PR #621 (open, draft, head `92e9272e`; recorded by BATCH-1111I, checked with `gh pr view 621`)** changes `e2e/support/gates.ts` and `e2e/ui-dark-primitives.spec.ts` only, no bound moves, and the owner gate MEDS-G-X1 is **answered 2026-11-12: "Merge #621 (Recommended)"** (recorded by BATCH-1112A). #621 may merge after that batch (head was `92e9272e` when answered; `532d8a27` on 2026-11-12 after L0 updated the branch, `gh pr view 621`, still a draft). **B3 (make G3 a required check) follows the merge**: an owner-side command in HUMAN-BLOCKERS B3. **Done (BATCH-1112C): #621 merged `7c7ab592` and #640 merged `dddf95b5`; the owner ran the B3 command on 2026-11-12 (owner-decisions B3-DONE), so G3 on Production Build is now a required check.** Option I is ended.

It is rare, but this switch shows whether stricter safety is on, and it would stay wrong for the 5-minute staleTime. Fix as the reviewer says: a click count in a ref, incremented in `setStrict`, captured in `onError`, and the write happens only if it has not moved; this replaces `!inFlight.current`, and the drop at `:79` is kept. Here a server value is known and the switch shows it, so it is not that state. The switch stays enabled and "Couldn't load" is true of that refetch; disabling a working switch on a transient failure would not make the shown value more correct, and the server stays the source of truth on the next PATCH. The existing test stays. This is L0's reading of delta item 4; the owner may overrule it. ## Planned 2026-11-01, harness speedup

Planned 2026-11-01 on `5e4c2e8a` by the Opus planner, from L0's measured analysis of 2026-11-01. 18 build-item runs took about 12.7 agent-hours and 10.8M tokens, and 8 reached a ready PR. Trivial rows paid the full pipeline: UIG002-TITLE took 35 min, SHELL-NAVREF-DEAD 37 min. 5 of 10 stops were harness friction and 3 were file-list gaps. Docs-only PRs wait about 10 min in CI. **Plan:** [2026-11-01-harness-speedup-plan.md](../2026-11-01-harness-speedup-plan.md). **Owner approved** #1 (path-scoped tests and CI) and #3 (small-row fast lane) under SPEEDUP-1-3, and BUILD-CONTINUE-1101 says keep building (both answered 2026-11-01, [owner-decisions](../../capstone-report/owner-decisions-2026-09-27.md)). #2 (L0 preflight), #4 (planner checklist) and #5 (rulings as PR comments, batched docs) need no approval: they are in [RUNBOOK §10.1](RUNBOOK.md#101-claude-code) from this docs PR. HARNESS-SPEEDUP wires #4 into the build-program planner prompt and adds the build-item half of #2. **Order:** HARNESS-SPEEDUP first, because every later item benefits: then RSI-OWNER-NOTES (resume #439), INTERP-WITHHOLD-MSG Task 3 (#433), RSI rows E-H, and the quiet-box chain (TOAST-SHELL, POPOVER-OPEN-COST, ...). PR retitles are done by L0 from the main session (BUILD-CONTINUE-1101, RUNBOOK §10.1 preflight 1).

So today a WBC, HbA1c or glucose row finds no knowledge-base entry: no description, no `high_interpretation`, no knowledge-base `critical_low` or `critical_high` (`interpret.py:227-234` and `:994-1001` pass them to `check_critical_value`; `:684-687` uses them for severity), no interventions. HbA1c has no entry in `DEFAULT_CRITICAL_THRESHOLDS` (`modules/interpret_safety.py:81-88`), so its seeded `critical_high` 10.0 (`:280`) is the only HbA1c limit and it has never been active. Lipid panel: the panel key is `lipid` (`api/observations.py:92`, passed as `panel_name` at `modules/interpret.py:437` and `:1141`), but the five relationship rows are seeded as `lipid_panel` (`:718`...), and `get_panel_relationships` matches exactly (`knowledge_loader.py:347`), so the Total/HDL and LDL/HDL ratio insights (`interpret.py:1149-1181`) never attach; the observation names in those rows (`ldl_cholesterol`, `hdl_cholesterol`, `total_cholesterol`) already match the stored names. The `thyroid` rows already match; the `diabetes` row matches no `PANEL_DEFINITIONS` key (4 panels) and is a named exception, left as is. **Which database, which chain:** the knowledge tables are in the **master** database (`models/knowledge_base.py` header: "stored in the master database"; `BiomarkerKnowledge` `:24`, `InterventionMapping` `:110`, `BiomarkerRelationship` `:185`; migration `001_initial` creates `biomarker_knowledge`; `main.py:49-54` runs `run_master_migrations_async()` and then `seed_all(skip_db_init=True)`), so the fix is a **master-chain** migration (`migrations/master/versions/`, new revision after `002_backup_schedules`, linear `down_revision`), not a profile migration (CLAUDE.md dual migrations). **Existing installs:** the seed skips an existing biomarker by name (`:952-965`; `_TEXT_REVISIONS` only rewrites text on a row whose exact old text matches, it cannot rename), so renaming the seed alone would leave the old row and add a second, renamed one, and `biomarker_knowledge.analyte_canonical` is unique (`models/knowledge_base.py:44-45`). The migration therefore runs first (migrations run before the seed, `main.py:49` then `:54`) and does the data fix: `UPDATE` the three biomarker names, the interventions' `analyte_canonical` (`hemoglobin_a1c` to `hba1c`) and the relationships' `primary_analyte`, `related_analyte` and `panel_name`; if a row with the new name already exists (a collision with the unique column), keep that one and delete the old row; every statement matches on the old value, so a second run changes nothing; no row is inserted. The seed then finds the renamed rows and skips them; the seed's `_TEXT_REVISIONS` keys follow the rename so a row still holding an old default text is revised as before. The standalone script (`seed_knowledge_base.py:1069-1073`, `init_database()` only, no migrations) on an unmigrated old database would add renamed rows beside the old ones: the implementer states in the PR body whether that path is guarded (for example by running the same rename statements at the top of `seed_all`) or accepted. **Frontend (one line):** `src/frontend/src/pages/ExplainAssistant.tsx:391-392` maps `hemoglobin_a1c: 'A1C'` and `glucose_fasting: 'Fasting Glucose'`, but the chips are built from stored names (`:400`), so a stored `hba1c` falls through to the capitalised fallback and the chip reads "How's my Hba1c?". Change the key on `:391` to `hba1c` (one line); `glucose` is already mapped at `:393`, so `:392` is dead and stays. If it is more than one line, a separate bullet. **Acceptance:** tests go through the real path, `NormalizeModule.normalize_analyte`, then `KnowledgeLoader.get_biomarker_knowledge`, then `interpret.py`'s context and result, on a database built the way main builds it (master migrations, then seed): **RED first** that WBC, HbA1c and glucose rows load their knowledge-base text; a test that every seeded `analyte_canonical` (biomarkers and interventions) and every relationship analyte is a name `normalize_analyte` can produce for some input, or a named exception in the test with a reason; the lipid ratio insights attach to the lipid panel through `interpret_panel`'s path (Total/HDL and LDL/HDL present when both values exist); an HbA1c value of 10.5 reaches `check_critical_value` with the knowledge-base `critical_high` 10.0 and `requires_physician_review` true, 9.5 false; migration tests: an old-name database is renamed with the same row count, a second run is a no-op, a database holding both an old and a new row ends with one, and `seed_all` afterwards adds no duplicate (counts read back); `modules/interpret_safety.py` unedited; every text that becomes reachable (each field of each newly matching analyte, per analyte, and the interventions) passes `validate_interpretation`'s prohibited patterns, the output pasted; the build must not change a word of seeded text (a test compares every seeded text field before and after to the pre-rename values). **Merge gate:** the PR body lists **verbatim** every text a patient can newly see (each field, per analyte, plus the interventions) and the limits that become active (WBC 2.0 and 30.0, HbA1c critical high 10.0, glucose 50.0 and 400.0 from the knowledge base alongside the existing defaults), for the owner's yes (KB-CONTENT-YES). Nothing merges before it. Notes for the owner: the glucose entry is titled "Fasting Glucose" and its text is fasting-specific, and after the rename it applies to every `glucose` row, fasting or not; the normalizer stores "Glucose, Fasting" as `glucose,_fasting`, which stays unmatched (candidate bullet ANALYTE-NAME-SWEEP). (5) No second copy of the card logic: reuse the path `force_regenerate=true` already takes. Found 2026-11-03: a vite server left behind by a command kept pytest slot 2 held and stalled a quiet run for about 4 h, because the command inherits the lock file descriptor. Evidence (BATCH-1103D author, a scratch copy of the script on `d1f05349` with its lock path moved, `PYSLOTS=1`, command `bash -c 'sleep 20 & exit 0'`, printed): without `-o`, a second `flock -n -E 75` on the slot after the script exited returned 75 (still held); with `-o` it returned 0, and the script's own exit code was 0 both times. `flock -o` is `--close` in this util-linux. Acceptance: that two-line test pasted RED and GREEN against the real script with a scratch lock directory; exit codes still pass through (`pyslot.sh true` exits 0, `pyslot.sh false` exits 1) and a busy slot still waits and retries on 75; LF endings kept (`git ls-files --eol scripts/pyslot.sh` shows `i/lf w/lf`). The lock is still held by `flock` itself while the command runs; only a child the command leaves behind stops holding it. |

The fix: `_classify_severity` receives the observation's unit and the two callers pass it. Same unit (compared normalised: case and whitespace): compare as now. A different unit the KB entry's `unit_conversions` can convert: convert the value to the limit's unit, then compare. No unit, or a unit it cannot convert: **no critical classification**; fall through to the report's own range, which is in the report's unit. The range checks (`abnormal_*`, `borderline_*`) are unchanged. The direction of `unit_conversions` is read from the seed and the loader, not guessed (`seed_knowledge_base.py:90` has `{"mmol/L": 0.0259}`), and pinned by a test on a real KB entry. Tests first: same unit critical; converted unit critical; converted unit not critical; unknown unit never critical; missing unit never critical; range classification unchanged. The plan greps the tests that pin `_classify_severity` or critical badges and lists each one that must change and why; an edit to an existing test must end stricter. **Ask-first:** `modules/interpret_safety.py` stays unedited; if the fix needs it, stop, the owner sees that diff first. Before merge: the full backend suite, `test_kb_canonical_names.py`, a safety review, and this docs batch reaching main; `contracts.md` :128 and :82 are handled by BATCH-1105D (see below), the head literals when #486 merges. **L0 rulings on #486, recorded by BATCH-1105D; this is the licence that must reach `origin/main` before #486 merges (rule 8).** **Defect on main** (L0's check, ruling 6673478599; lines re-read on `origin/main` at `8c1f0948`): observations are stored as the lab printed them (`models/observation.py:61-63`), the badge compares the raw value with the KB limit (`modules/interpret.py:894-897`) and the review banner passes the raw value to `check_critical_value` (`modules/interpret.py:246-255`; default table `modules/interpret_safety.py:82-90`, glucose 50 and 400). So a glucose of 7.0 mmol/L gets `critical_low` and the banner "Critical low value: 7.0 is below critical threshold of 50.0". **Licensed files:** `be/modules/interpret.py` and the PR's own test file `be/tests/test_kb_canonical_names.py` (HC-KCN-010 to 013), nothing else. `modules/interpret_safety.py` is **not** licensed and is not in the diff (comment 6676747221: the exact diff at `9a6ece3d` is 288 lines of `interpret.py`, `interpret_safety.py` untouched); `modules/normalize.py`, the seeded text and the limits are not touched. No existing main test is edited. **The rule** (6673478599 D1 to D3, as amended by 6675742258 and 6676366119; the owner answers CRIT-BANNER-UNITS, SAVED-SEVERITY-REBUILD, CRIT-COUNT-UNITS and CRIT-BANNER-WORDING are in owner-decisions): (1) **Conversion source.** `modules/normalize.py` `convert_to_canonical`, the app's one tested table ("NEVER guess a factor"), not the KB's `unit_conversions_json`; the table is not widened. A helper `value_in_kb_unit` gives the value in the unit the KB's critical limits use, or nothing. **Nothing, or a missing unit, means no critical: no badge and no banner**, so badge and banner always agree (owner: "Same rule as badge"). The range checks are unchanged. (2) **Same unit** after normalising (case, whitespace): compared as is. **Same-entry units:** when the KB `standard_unit` is itself a listed variant in the same `normalize.py` table entry, convert to canonical and then into the KB unit with that entry's own factor (TSH 12 µIU/mL is 12 mIU/L, `critical_high`); L0's own first licence (D1) missed this and would have dropped TSH. (3) **Cell counts** (owner: "Treat listed spellings same"), for `white_blood_cells` and `platelets` only, in `interpret.py` and not in `normalize.py`, so trends do not change: `x10E3/uL`, `x10^3/uL`, `10*3/uL`, `K/uL`, `10^3/µL`, `10³/µL`, `10^9/L` and `x10^9/L` count as the KB's `10^3/uL` (factor 1); `cells/uL` is divided by 1000; any other spelling: no critical. (4) **Exact mass-concentration units** (6676366119 B, L0, inside the owner's "convert" answer): when the KB unit is `mg/dL`, `mg%` is `mg/dL` (factor 1) and `g/L` is `mg/dL` times 100, in `interpret.py` beside the cell-count list. (5) **Banner wording** (owner: "Show both units"; 6675742258 §4, polish 6676100146 and 6676366119 A, C, D): the patient's number and unit first, as printed, with no trailing `.0`; the converted number rounded to one decimal under 100 and a whole number from 100, with up to two more decimals if it would print equal to the threshold; thresholds without a trailing `.0`, with the KB unit (the count unit prints as `x10^3/uL`). Whether to print the parenthesis and the patient-unit threshold is decided from the factor (`value_in_kb_unit(info, 1.0, unit) == 1.0`), not from the value, so a value of 0 still gets it. Sentences: `Critical low value: 40 mg/dL is below the critical threshold of 50 mg/dL.` (same unit, as pinned by the test file at `9a6ece3d`); `Critical high value: 900 µmol/L (10.2 mg/dL) is above the critical threshold of 10 mg/dL.` (converted); `Critical low value: 1.5 K/uL is below the critical threshold of 2 K/uL.` (factor 1: no parenthesis, threshold in the patient's unit); `cells/uL` keeps the parenthesis. Built caller-side in `interpret.py`. (6) **Saved cards** (owner: "Rebuild on read"; 6673478599 D3, narrowed by 6673894287 and 6673944163, final form 6675742258 §3 and 6676366119 B): a saved template-written card is rebuilt on read when it counts as critical (its `severity_level` starts with `critical`, or `requires_physician_review` is true; for template cards that flag is set only by the critical check) or the new result is critical, and the unit is missing, or differs from the KB unit after normalising and the value converts, and the new result differs. A present unit that cannot be converted never downgrades a saved critical. A same-unit difference is a KB revision and is not stale (`test_hc_vrc_023_a_knowledge_base_card_whose_text_has_changed_is_not_stale` stays unedited, and the 41 `test_value_range_compare.py` fixtures stay unedited). **Model-written cards are left alone** (owner VALUE-RANGE-SAVED-CARDS: the model is never called on a read). **Reviews and tests:** an Opus safety review at `2d406d48` found 2 BLOCKER and 1 MAJOR (correct TSH, WBC and platelet criticals lost; D3 rebuilding saved ones into non-critical; the commonest wrong saved card, a mmol/L glucose with a "Critical low" banner but an `abnormal_high` badge, missed; banner with float noise and no unit), fixed in `a5d870b8` and `62e97be0`; the re-review at `62e97be0` confirmed them fixed, found no rebuild loop, log noise or audit gap, and all 14 deliberate breaks fail at least one test, including the model-path banner; its four fixes (A to D above) are in `9a6ece3d`. HC-KCN-010 to 013 (20 test functions at `9a6ece3d`) are in the PR's own test file; the exact diff and the banner sentences went to the owner (KB-UNIT-DIFF, 6676747221). **Known limits of KB-UNIT-BADGE** (6675742258, 6676366119; the owner is told; each is a case where no critical is shown, or an old card is not corrected): (a) a missing unit is never critical, by the owner's rule; (b) HbA1c in mmol/mol is never critical (the relation is affine, not a factor, and is not in the table), including an old saved `hemoglobin_a1c` critical card (manual entry only); (c) TSH in `mU/L` or `mIU/mL` (the extractor cannot produce them) is never critical; (d) WBC in `x10E9/L`, `G/L` or `M/uL` is never critical; (e) creatinine in `mmol/L` and hemoglobin in `mmol/L` are never critical (glucose in `mg%` and `g/L` was on this list in 6675742258 and is converted since 6676366119 B); (f) correct criticals saved on main keep main's old banner wording until the card is regenerated; (g) model-written cards with a wrong critical badge are not rebuilt; (h) the no-KB-row path (potassium, sodium) still compares raw values against the default table, which is behaviour on main: bullet CRIT-DEFAULT-TABLE-UNITS. **Related candidate bullets:** BADGE-STYLE-MAP (the frontend badge map never matches the backend's words), CRIT-DEFAULT-TABLE-UNITS. **L0's error, recorded:** L0's D1 "no critical when it cannot convert" was too narrow; the Opus safety review caught it (recurring-failures mode 2). |

Step one is done for two paths by the planner on `c0f23fc9`, output in the plan: `python -m uvicorn main:app` (the command `dev.ps1:816`, `:922`, `dev.sh:261` and `start-backend.mjs:56` run) logged `"GET /api/v1/search/?q=PLANNERPROBEterm HTTP/1.1" 401 Unauthorized`, and `python main.py` (`uvicorn.run` at `main.py:188`, reloader on) logged `"GET /api/v1/search/?q=PLANNERPROBEmainpy HTTP/1.1" 401 Unauthorized`; the line is written before auth, so a logged-out request leaks too. Where it lands: `dev.sh:261` writes `logs/dev-backend.log` on disk; `dev.ps1:816`/`:922` start a hidden window with no redirect, so the lines go to that console only (L0 confirms on Windows: the build-item cannot run `dev.ps1`); `start-backend.mjs` inherits stdio, so e2e and CI logs carry it. `SECURITY_AUDIT` (`security/audit_middleware.py:46`) and the rate-limit line log `scope["path"]`, which has no query string; the search audit row (`api/search.py:106-120`) holds no term. Acceptance: a term sent as `?q=` never appears in any record of any logger nor in the formatted access line, on an in-process `uvicorn.Server` and on both subprocess launch paths, while the method, path and status still appear (the access log is kept); the filter survives alembic's `fileConfig` (`migrations/profile/env.py:53`). **Stay unedited and green:** `tests/monitoring/test_correlation.py`, `tests/security/test_audit_middleware.py`, `test_search.py`. **For L0, not in scope:** analyte names in path segments (`/observations/trends/{analyte}`), and Vite's `http proxy error: <url>` line in `logs/dev-frontend.log` when the backend is down. **L0 security review of #511 (privacy lens; comment 6680689815, 2026-11-06; BATCH-1106A): pass at head `9eb0cf16`.** The scrub is a filter on the `uvicorn.access` logger, not on a handler, so stdlib `dictConfig` and `fileConfig` (which remove handlers, not logger filters) leave it in place; it is installed in `create_app()`, which the `--reload` worker runs on import of `main:app`. Other paths checked: `modules/search.py:458` logs the FTS5 `SQLAlchemyError`, but both engines set `hide_parameters=True` (`core/database.py:64`, `core/profile_database.py:329`) and `_fts_expression` passes only quoted `\w+` tokens, so the term cannot realistically reach it (not changed); no other product `logger.*` call logs `request.url` or `query_params`. Log destinations: the app has no file handlers; `dev.sh:261` writes uvicorn output to `logs/dev-backend.log` and the scrub covers it; `dev.ps1:815` and `:921` start the backend with `-WindowStyle Hidden` and no redirect, so the Windows launcher writes no backend log, and `dev.ps1` is unchanged by this PR. Reviewer MINORs (WebSocket lines on `uvicorn.error`; no WS routes exist) accepted as known limits. **Owner answer 2026-11-06, "Code reading is enough (Recommended)"** for the Windows path (owner-decisions, row SEARCH-LOG-SCRUB Windows path): #511 can merge once CI passes and it is marked ready. Note from L0: the classifier refused `gh pr ready 511` for both the workflow and L0, so marking it ready is still to do. |

**Owner gates for 1111G, all ANSWERED 2026-11-11 (the owner answered all four in one message to L0: "1111G gates: all Recommended"; recorded by BATCH-1111H in owner-decisions; the questions and options below are verbatim from the planner's draft). Chosen: AVA-OLD-ROWS "(a) Stop writing it; leave old rows (Recommended)"; VERIFY-NOOP "(a) Skip what is done; refuse an empty undo (Recommended)"; MEM-PURGE "(a) Clear both at lock, logout, delete and restore (Recommended)"; LEGEND-LIST "(a) Skip lines whose name is a label word (Recommended)":**

**AVA-OLD-ROWS (row AUDIT-VIEW-ANALYTE)**

**Question.** Opening a trend chart or a lab panel writes an audit row into the unencrypted master database, and that row stores the test's name (for example `hiv_viral_load`, or the panel `thyroid`) as its entity id (measured). A row is written only for a test the patient really has results for. The audit module says test names are never stored there. The fix stops writing the name from now on. What about rows already written?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Stop writing it; leave old rows (Recommended)** | New rows store `all` (as Search and Timeline do) with the action and count. Rows already on disk keep the name until the profile is deleted. No master-DB migration. |
| (b) Stop writing it and scrub old rows | Also a master Alembic migration that rewrites `entity_id` to `all` on old `observation.view` rows with action `trend` or `panel`. Rewrites audit history, which is itself an audit question. |
| (c) Leave it | Test names stay in the unencrypted database. |

Why (a): it stops the leak with a two-line change and does not rewrite audit history. It is the smaller change, not the more private one: names already written stay in the unencrypted database until the profile is deleted. (b) is recorded as a follow-up row (AVA-SCRUB-OLD) either way.

**VERIFY-NOOP (rows DOC-VERIFY-ALL-KEEPS and VERIFY-UNDO-NOOP)**

**Question.** Two verify actions rewrite rows that are already in the state asked for (both measured):
- **Verify all** on a document marks every row checked again: a row the patient checked last week gets today's date and a new version number, so the record of when it was really checked is lost.
- **Undo verify** on a row that is not verified still bumps its version and writes an "unverified" audit row.

What should a repeat do?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Skip what is done; refuse an empty undo (Recommended)** | Verify all checks only the rows not yet checked; already-checked rows (and an already-verified document) keep their date and version, and the document's audit row counts only the rows it checked. Undo on an unchecked row is refused ("not verified") and changes nothing. |
| (b) Skip what is done; silent undo | As (a), but Undo on an unchecked row answers OK and returns the row unchanged. That answer shows the row's values without an audit row, which the audit rule does not allow, so it would also need a "viewed" audit row. |
| (c) Leave both | Today's behaviour. |

Why (a): it keeps the real "checked on" dates, never records an action that did not happen, leaves no unaudited read, and changes nothing a patient sees (no screen offers Undo yet).

**MEM-PURGE (row PROFILE-MEMORY-PURGE)**

**Question.** Lock, logout, profile delete and restore already clear a profile's exports from memory. Two other in-memory stores are not cleared (read from the code): rendered report pages (the decrypted page pictures behind Source Trace, kept for 5 minutes but only swept on the next page request, so they can stay much longer on an idle app) and the assistant's cached answers (kept until the app restarts). Should those be cleared at the same moments?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Clear both at lock, logout, delete and restore (Recommended)** | Both are dropped for that profile only, in the same step that clears exports. After unlock, the first repeat question is answered fresh (slower once). |
| (b) Clear page pictures only | Answers stay cached across lock; they hold lab-derived text. |
| (c) Leave it | Both stay in memory after lock. |

Why (a): a locked app should hold no decrypted patient content; the cost is one fresh answer after unlock.

**LEGEND-LIST (row EXTRACT-LEGEND-LINES)**

**Question.** When a lab PDF is read, lines that are not results but look like one, such as "Target: 100 mg/dL", "Goal: 7.0 %", "Optimal 100 mg/dL", "Normal: 5.7 %" or "Reference range: 70 mg/dL", become result rows named "Target", "Goal" and so on (measured). They then sit in the verify queue as if they were tests. What should the reader do with them?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Skip lines whose name is a label word (Recommended)** | A line whose whole name is one of these is never read as a test: target, goal, optimal, desirable, recommended, reference, reference range, normal, normal range, therapeutic range. Real results ("Glucose 95 mg/dL", "Target Cells 2 %") are unchanged. Two-part labels such as "LDL Target: 100 mg/dL" still get through (a later row if wanted). A lab whose test is really called one of these words would be missed; none is known. |
| (b) Keep them, marked low confidence | They stay in the verify queue, sorted last. |
| (c) Leave it | They keep appearing as results. |

Why (a): a target is not a result, and showing it as one is the riskier error; the list is short and fixed.

| AUDIT-READ-ROUTES task 3 | E | PRODUCT (backend; privacy, audit; security lens) | **HELD (no build):** `set_llm_provider` (`be/api/model_settings.py:921-981`) writes no audit row and HC-ARR-013 is absent (checked on `89c3811e`); it needs `be/tests/test_audit_order_guard.py`, which parked #264 and #239 hold | L0 ruling PROVIDER-AUDIT (a), already answered (see the 2026-11-06 docs-audit plan) | — | `be/api/model_settings.py`, `be/tests/test_audit_read_routes.py`, `be/tests/test_audit_order_guard.py` (CLASSIFICATION row) | — | — | **held** (plan: [AUDIT-READ-ROUTES Task 3 (held)](../2026-11-11-batch-1111i-plan.md#audit-read-routes-task-3-held)). Frees when #264 and #239 close or merge. |

**Owner gates for 1111I, all ANSWERED 2026-11-12 (asked in one prompt of four; answers: RESTORE-FAIL-PURGE "Leave it", RECOVER-AUDIT-WORDING "Leave it", TEST-RESET-ORDER "Close as accepted (Recommended)", DISCLAIMER-APPEND "Fix the guard"; recorded by BATCH-1112A in owner-decisions; the questions and options below are verbatim from the planner's `owner-gates-1111i.md`):**

**RESTORE-FAIL-PURGE (row MEM-PURGE-RESTORE-FAIL)**
**Question.** A restore closes the profile's vault before it touches any file. When the restore then fails partway (the vault and key files were already replaced from the backup) or fails outright, the profile's cached exports stay in memory. After PROFILE-MEMORY-PURGE, so do its page pictures and cached answers. Today only a successful restore clears them. Should every restore outcome clear them once the vault closes?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Clear as soon as the vault closes, every outcome (Recommended)** | The clear moves to just after the vault closes, so success, partial failure, failure and a restore that errors out all clear memory. A refused restore (wrong password, wrong phrase, corrupt backup) closes nothing and still keeps them (pinned by HC-ERP-003). |
| (b) Clear on partial failure only | Partial failure is the case where the data changed. A plain failure keeps the pre-restore exports in memory while the vault is closed. |
| (c) Leave it | A failed or partial restore keeps them in memory until the next lock, logout or expiry. |

Why (a): closing the vault is a lock in effect, and MEM-PURGE (a) already said a locked profile keeps no decrypted content in memory. It is one moved line in `api/backup.py`; the audit rows and error texts are unchanged.

**RECOVER-AUDIT-WORDING (row RECOVER-AUDIT-PRE-ROTATE)**
**Question.** Recovering with a recovery code writes the audit row "recovered" with "recovery_rotated: true" and commits it before the recovery code is actually rotated (the order you chose in RECOVER-ORDER-COMMIT). If the rotation then fails, the request returns an error, the new password works, the old code still works, and the audit row says the code was rotated. Change the wording?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Stop claiming it in the first row (Recommended)** | The "recovered" row records only "password_reset: true". The rotation is recorded by the second row ("recovery code generated, after recovery"), which is written only after the rotation succeeds. Nothing else in recovery changes. |
| (b) Write "recovery_rotated: false" first, then true on the second row | The same truth, with one more field to read. |
| (c) Leave it | On a rotation failure the audit trail overstates what happened. |

Why (a): the audit trail then never claims a step that did not run, the order you chose stays exactly as it is, and the change is one line in `api/profiles.py` (auth code, hence this question). A test-strength concern raised on the same code (HC-RECOV-030b) turned out to be covered by HC-RECOV-030 (measured), so it needs no change.

**TEST-RESET-ORDER (row TEST-RESET-PARTIAL)**
**Question.** The Playwright-only "reset synthetic test profile" route (404 in production) deletes the profile's document files before it commits the database reset. If that commit fails, the files are gone and no audit row is written. The only reorder that keeps the current tests produces the opposite half-state (database reset, files left, no audit row). What should happen?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Leave it; close as accepted (Recommended)** | No change. The route only runs for synthetic Playwright profiles; a retry, which Playwright setup does anyway, completes either half-state. |
| (b) Commit first, then delete files; on a file failure after the commit, write a "partial" audit row | A failed commit deletes nothing, and a later file failure is audited as partial. One existing test (HC-RESET-041, "no audit row on failure") is re-pinned to allow the partial row. |

Why (a): it is a test-only route with no patient data; (b) touches `api/profiles.py` and changes a pinned audit rule for little gain.

**DISCLAIMER-APPEND (row DISCLAIMER-GUARD-QUIRK)**
**Question.** The safety guard adds its standard disclaimer only when an explanation has none of its three disclaimer signals (the word "consult"; the words "healthcare provider", "doctor" or "physician"; the words "educational" or "informational"). It then requires two of the three. So an explanation with exactly one signal, for example "Your healthcare provider can review it.", gets no disclaimer added and is withheld (the patient sees the "talk to your clinician" card instead). No built-in text triggers this today (measured: every seeded card passes), and existing tests already pin the behaviour. Change the guard?

| Option | What happens |
|---|---|
| **(a) Leave the guard; close as known (Recommended)** | No change to the medical-safety module. A one-signal text keeps being withheld, which fails safe. Built-in texts keep two signals, as the existing tests require. |
| (b) Add the disclaimer whenever fewer than two signals are present | Such cards show with the standard disclaimer appended instead of being withheld. It edits `interpret_safety.py`, and two tests that use a one-signal text to force a withhold need a new fixture. It would get its own plan. |

Why (a): the quirk only withholds, it never lets unsafe text through, and no built-in text triggers it (knowledge rows a user edited were not measured). (b) is a medical-safety change with no current patient-visible case.
